### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

### Page 7

セン

◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.

## Document 433: Why hasn’t there been a major financial crisis in recent years_ (2026-11-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Why hasn’t there been a major financial crisis in recent years_ (2026-11-03).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 19
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Why hasn’t there been a major 
financial crisis in recent years?

1 
Authors 
Henry Allen 
Macro Strategist

Peter Sidorov, CFA 
Senior Economist

Camilla Siazon 
Research Analyst

November 3, 2026 
— The economist Hyman Minsky warned, in essence, that “stability is destabilising”. He 
argued that a lengthy period of financial stability could be problematic, as it induced 
risk-taking and complacency amongst investors, laying the foundation for the next 
crisis. For example, lenders relax their terms and borrowers are more willing to extend 
themselves, as nobody wants to miss out. This dynamic makes economies inherently 
prone to financial panics and boom-bust cycles. 
— But since the 2008 crisis, and the subsequent sovereign crisis in the Eurozone, we 
have yet to see another financial crisis happen on a scale that had a lasting market 
and macroeconomic impact. There have been clear moments of concern, as in early 
2023 with the regional bank turmoil, but nothing that has caused a lasting impact. So 
this led us to ask why. 
— In this paper, we examine why the post-GFC era has seen fewer financial crises. 
Explanations include the reforms made after 2008 to avoid a repeat, the lower 
proportion of financial assets held by the banking system, as well as the speedier 
reaction of policymakers when wobbles have occurred. We then consider a few 
candidates that could cause the next crisis, including AI vulnerabilities, private credit, 
and stretched fiscal positions. 
— Ultimately, financial crises haven’t been abolished, but the reforms of recent years 
have made it harder for shocks to propagate through the traditional banking system. 
Moreover, policymakers have become far more inclined to intervene when initial signs 
of stress have emerged.

### Page 2

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 1: What is a financial crisis, and what causes them?
There isn’t a single technical definition of a “financial crisis”, and they come in many
different types. Commonly, we think of banking crises like 2008, but they can also
encompass currency/balance of payments crises, debt crises and other forms too.
Given currency and sovereign crises have been more common in emerging markets
(the UK's ERM crisis in 1992 and the euro crisis being the two most notable DM

occurrences), historical analysis in developed economies has tended to focus
heavily on banking crises. Non-bank funding or market-liquidity crises are often not
categorised separately in the literature, but they have become more common in
recent years. This includes the "dash-for-cash" stress at the onset of the Covid
pandemic in March 2020 and the 2022 LDI crisis in the UK.
But for all the different types of financial crisis, there are common themes between

them. Often, they are accompanied by asset bubbles that burst in a non-linear
fashion. We generally see a severe tightening in credit conditions, which in turn
flows through into a broader economic contraction. Frequently, there are
contagion-like dynamics that can be difficult to stop. And in the modern world, they
usually require policymakers to step in, stabilising the situation with interventions.
At risk of oversimplification, we note four factors that determine the emergence and

the severity of a financial crisis. The first three are well-established in the financial
literature, sometimes pooled together into a build-up and a crisis phase
(Brunnermeier & Oehmke 2013), while the fourth has become increasingly
important over the past 15-20 years:
1.  Underlying imbalances. Bubbles and financial imbalances build during
the run-up phase, leaving accumulated risks that materialise when the
crisis erupts. This is the build-up typically described by the early phases of

Kindleberger's model of financial crises.
2.  Trigger. This is the shock that turns an existing vulnerability into clear
financial stress. These are often very difficult to predict and we mostly leave
these outside the focus of this report.
3.  Amplification and financial system fragility. Various factors determine
whether a shock propagates through the financial system, and the
intensity of that shock. These include leverage, liquidity and maturity

mismatches, interconnectedness, and the concentration of exposures.
Other channels in the financial system can then amplify the shock,
including bank runs, fire sales and margin calls, and negative net worth
effects, sovereign–bank loops etc.
4.  Intervention/policy response. This has been increasingly important in
recent decades as the size and scope of policy response to crises has
increased. The outright size can vary, and intervention can occur across

several dimensions, ranging from direct intervention in financial markets,
to counter-cyclical macroeconomic policy that mitigates the impact on the
real economy.
Clearly, these factors are not independent and the interaction between them is what
determines the severity of a financial crisis.

### Page 3

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
One important aspect is the joint role that underlying imbalances and amplification
play. While theoretical models such as Diamond & Dybvig highlight the potential for
a bank run that triggers a broad crisis, empirical research shows that in practice, it
is exceedingly rare for a bank run to lead to bank failure in the absence of an
underlying solvency issue (Correia, Luck and Verner 2026). In other words, the

presence of amplification channels alone does not lead to a crisis.
Conversely, in the absence of amplification, it is possible to get significant
turbulence in asset prices, but without a financial crisis. For instance, a bear market,
wider credit spreads, or even a recession do not in and of themselves imply there
is a financial crisis. This was seen clearly with the bursting of the dot com bubble,
a severe equity decline but not a financial crisis, though it did cause a recession. At

the same time, in the chart below, we can see several US equity bear markets that
took place outside of recessions. On other occasions, however, a “Minsky
Moment” can then happen, where even a seemingly minor event can trigger a high-
profile incident that cascades across the financial system.
Figure 1: S&P 500 drawdowns over time, with shaded recessions showing select episodes when the index fell over 20%
without coinciding with a recession
Source :Bloomberg Finance LP, NBER, Deutsche Bank Research

Looking back through history, we can see how variation in these factors has led to
different regimes over time in terms of frequency/severity of financial factors.

### Page 4

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 2. Why have financial crises been more or less likely
in certain periods of history?
1. Pre-WWII: More frequent financial instability, albeit under a very
different system.
Financial instability was a recurrent feature of the pre-WWII era. During the 19th
and early 20th centuries, there were frequent panics that often led to major slumps.
For instance, the Panic of 1873 sparked a period often known as the “Long

Depression”, which extended into the 1890s for many countries.
In part, financial panics occurred more frequently because policy operated under
very different settings. For instance, the Gold Standard as the monetary system
constrained a countercyclical policy response and transmitted deflation
internationally, while falling prices increased real debt burdens. Moreover, the
Federal Reserve wasn’t established until 1913, and the lack of an effective lender of

last resort meant there was a private intervention during the US Panic of 1907. The
first wave of globalisation also brought new vulnerabilities. Between 1870 and
1913, many emerging economies accumulated debts denominated in gold or
foreign currency, leaving them exposed when capital inflows reversed or exchange
rates weakened.
The biggest and most famous crisis occurred in 1929, when the Wall Street Crash
of that year sparked the Great Depression. The length and depth of that crisis helped

to stimulate the study of modern macroeconomics, with huge efforts to prevent a
repeat over the years ahead. Indeed, the failures of the response to the Great
Depression played a large role in the much swifter response to the 2008 financial
crisis, helping to avoid a second Great Depression.
That experience established much of the modern financial safety net that exists to
this day. For instance in the US, the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation was

established in 1933. The US and many other countries came off the Gold Standard
as well in this period. There were also regulatory reforms, and the Glass-Steagall Act
separated out commercial from investment banking.
2. 1944-1971: The Bretton Woods era coincided with fewer financial
crises
Around the end of WWII, economic policymakers sought to create an international
monetary system for the post-war era. The relative financial calm that followed

reflected a combination of restrictions on international capital movement, tightly
regulated domestic financial systems, and a system of fixed exchange rates that
aimed to provide stability and prevent the competitive devaluations that occurred
in the 1930s. This saw exchange rates pegged against the US Dollar, which in turn
was convertible to gold at $35 per ounce.
At the same time, capital controls helped preserve monetary policy flexibility, while

maintaining the system of fixed exchange rates. These controls had several
downsides but they also insulated countries from “sudden stop” crises that can
occur, when capital rapidly flees a country. Moreover, capital controls prevented
the rapid buildup of cross-border systemic leverage. So it was more difficult to
generate a global financial crisis in the same way.

### Page 5

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
Figure 2: Gold price (USD/oz, log scale)
10

1920 1940 1960 1980 2000 2020
In the Bretton Woods
era, the dollar was tied
to gold
Source : Finaeon, Deutsche Bank
Alongside capital controls, there were also domestic reforms in the aftermath of the
Great Depression, which sought to avoid a repeat. As mentioned earlier, the US
Glass-Steagall Act of 1933 separated commercial banking from investment

banking. Another example of the era’s regulated financial system in the US was
Regulation Q, which imposed maximum interest rates that commercial banks
could pay on deposits. That meant depositors could not search for yield and cause
a run on deposits as easily. Another rationale was that with institutions not
competing for deposits, it would avoid a compression in the margin between the
rates banks paid on their lending and borrowing, which would disincentivise a push

into riskier investments.
Finally, economic growth was also stronger in this period, with the period between
WWII and the 1973 oil shock often described as the “Golden Age”. This played a role
in avoiding financial crises too, as faster growth helped keep debt default rates
down, avoiding the kind of “trigger” events above that might spark a financial crisis.
Coupled with financial repression, strong growth also helped to bring down the
high post-war public debt burdens.

Over time however, the peg between the dollar and gold found itself under growing
strain. The economist Robert Triffin identified the “Triffin dilemma”. Supplying the
world with dollar reserves resulted in outstanding dollar liabilities around the world
that exceeded the gold reserves in the US, weakening confidence that dollars could
ultimately be redeemed for gold. Conversely, restricting dollar supply to protect
those reserves and preserve convertibility risked depriving the system of the

liquidity needed to support trade and growth.
These tensions intensified in the late 1960s amid expansionary US policies,
including spending on the Vietnam War and Great Society programmes. The
continued drain in US gold reserves eventually led the US to suspend the
convertibility of the US Dollar into gold, effectively ending the Bretton Woods
system. Initially, there were attempts to bring some kind of fixed-exchange rate
system back, but the world of fiat currencies (with no link to a precious metal)

persists to this day.

### Page 6

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
3. 1971-2008: Financial crises become more frequent after the
breakdown of Bretton Woods to the Global Financial Crisis
After the breakdown of the Bretton Woods system, financial crises became a more
regular occurrence. While the end of the gold convertibility did not by itself trigger
future crises, it opened the door for wider financial liberalisation, with the resulting
rise in capital mobility and looser domestic financial regulation then helping to

facilitate a global build-up of imbalances.
The 1980s and 1990s were a period of frequent crisis events. The transition away
from capital controls meant that capital could flow more freely across borders.
However, this could also cause a “sudden stop” if investors suddenly pulled their
capital out of a country, contributing to bank runs and currency collapses.
Moreover, many emerging markets borrowed in foreign currencies like the US
Dollar, meaning that a depreciation in their own currency would see this USD-

denominated debt surge in local currency terms. This meant it was a particular
problem if US interest rates were increasing, as it became harder for many
emerging markets to repay their debts. This was a contributing factor behind the
Latin American debt crisis in the early 1980s. And the rate hikes also had domestic
ramifications, being a contributing factor behind the Savings and Loan crisis in the
United States.
With capital flowing more freely, that also played a role in the 1994 Mexican Peso

Crisis, and the 1997 Asian Financial Crisis. A sudden reversal in capital flows led to
crisis and ultimately recession in many countries. The following year, the Russian
financial crisis then took place, and fears grew in the United States about the wider
ramifications, as the hedge fund Long-Term Capital Management (LTCM) came
under pressure. Indeed, this period saw 75bps of pre-emptive rate cuts from the
Federal Reserve.
This volatility also saw the Fed proactively seek to step in and smooth out the

economic volatility. This was broadly successful, but in the process also helped
engender complacency and a further buildup of leverage. Indeed, after the dot com
bubble burst, the federal funds rate was cut to just 1% in mid-2003, where it
remained until mid-2004.
This period saw clear optimism that another Great Depression was highly unlikely.
For instance, Nobel Prize-winning economist Robert Lucas argued in his 2003 AEA
Presidential Address that “macroeconomics in this original sense has succeeded:

Its central problem of depression prevention has been solved, for all practical
purposes, and has in fact been solved for many decades.”
However, as we now know, the 2008 financial crisis occurred, driving the biggest
global downturn since the Great Depression of the 1930s. It turned out that Minsky
had been correct, and a lengthy period without a crisis had once again helped to
induce complacency, laying the foundations for a repeat.

### Page 7

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

Section 3: The Post-2008 world: Why have we not had
another big financial crisis?
Since the Global Financial Crisis of 2008, we have thankfully avoided a major
financial crisis. Despite clear moments of stress, most notably around the collapse
of SVB in March 2023, we have not seen a similar wave of bank collapses on that
scale. There are several components to this.
First, post-GFC deleveraging cleared out risks in the system, with countries and
sectors that had excess indebtedness before the GFC experiencing pronounced

declines in leverage.
Second, there have been reforms explicitly designed to avoid a repeat of 2008.
After the crisis happened, there was a clear recognition that gaps in regulation had
exacerbated the crisis, leading to effects that cascaded across the financial system.
For instance, the Basel III reforms required banks to hold more equity, with a
minimum requirement for Common Equity Tier 1 (CET1) capital. They also
introduced the liquidity coverage ratio, which requires banks to hold enough high-

quality liquid assets to ensure they can cover their net cash outflows under a 30-day
stressed scenario. In addition, the net stable funding ratio was brought in, requiring
banks to have sufficient stable funding that exceeded the required amount of stable
funding for a one-year stressed scenario.
At the national level, further reforms were also introduced. In the US, the Dodd-
Frank Act was introduced, with measures including the Volcker Rule that restricted

banks from engaging in proprietary trading, and required the Federal Reserve to
perform an annual stress test of large institutions. Meanwhile in the European
Union, which also experienced a sovereign debt crisis, reforms included a “Single
Supervisory Mechanism”, whereby banking supervision was brought under the
ECB, ensuring harmonised regulatory standards.

### Page 8

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
Third, the share of financial assets held by banks has fallen since the 2008 crisis.
This hasn’t eliminated risk, but commercial banks are funded by retail deposits that
can quickly fly. By contrast, non-bank financial institutions (NBFIs), e.g. hedge
funds, are not as prone to these bank run dynamics. For instance, a private credit
closed-ended fund is not one where investors can simply redeem their funds on

demand.
We can see this shift in the data. Looking at the FSB’s group of 21 plus the Eurozone,
we can see that the share of assets held by banks has fallen from a peak of 46% in
2008, to 38% in 2024. By contrast, the share held by NBFIs has risen from 43% to
51%.
Figure 5: Share of Assets of Financial Corporations (FSB 21 + EA Group)

Banks Nonbank Financial Intermediation Sector (NBFI)
Source :Haver Analytics, Deutsche Bank

Fourth, the lessons of 2008 have meant that when the financial system has had
wobbles since, policymakers have acted with incredible speed to stop the
turmoil.
This was most obvious when the Covid-19 pandemic began. Whilst a pandemic
was very different to a financial crisis, there was initially huge turmoil in financial
markets. Indeed, the S&P 500 fell by more than a third in just over a month, a decline
at a speed and scale unseen since the Great Depression. Meanwhile, US HY credit

spreads soared to levels last seen around the financial crisis, and at the height of the
turmoil, there was a huge spike in real yields, despite the economy entering a
recession.
Against this backdrop, central banks wasted no time in acting. For instance, the Fed
delivered an emergency 50bp rate cut, followed by another 100bp rate cut.
Moreover, they swiftly resumed Quantitative Easing, and announced the
establishment of multiple new facilities to ensure the smooth functioning of credit

markets. These included a Primary Market Corporate Credit Facility, to support new
bond and loan issuance, along with a Secondary Market Corporate Credit facility,
which offered liquidity for outstanding corporate bonds. Meanwhile, a third facility,
called the Term Asset-Backed Securities Loan Facility, was set up to enable the
issuance of asset-backed securities. Similarly in Europe, the ECB set up a new
Pandemic Emergency Purchase Programme (PEPP), which ultimately had a total

envelope of €1.85tn.
Whilst the pandemic caused a huge economic shock, the swift action meant it did
not translate into a financial one, and the brief tightening in credit conditions was

### Page 9

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
unwound over the coming months.
A similar pattern was seen during the regional banking turmoil of March 2023. This
was a financial panic, which initially began with the collapse of Silicon Valley Bank.
However, this began to spread, and Signature Bank also failed. Then in Europe,
Credit Suisse came under investor scrutiny and saw large deposit outflows, which
culminated in a purchase by UBS that included guarantees from the Swiss

government. This coincided with huge market moves, with the 2yr Treasury yield
posting its biggest daily decline in over 40 years, with a 61bps decline on March 13.
However, policymakers swiftly moved to stop any contagion. The US Treasury,
Federal Reserve and the FDIC released a joint statement stating that all deposits at
SVB would be protected, and that the Federal Reserve would provide additional
funding to ensure banks could meet the needs of depositors. Meanwhile, the Fed’s

balance sheet briefly expanded again, despite the fact it had begun the process of
quantitative tightening. These actions ultimately meant the crisis proved contained
and did not lead to a wave of further bank collapses in the manner of 2008.

Fifth, policymakers have also responded to recent shocks with huge fiscal

transfers, meaning that debt has migrated from private towards sovereign
balance sheets.
Whilst global debt has risen slightly over the last decade, if you dig deeper into the
numbers, that’s entirely driven by a rise in government debt. If we compare the
statistics for Q2 2026, relative to Q2 2016, there’s actually been a decline in debt for
households, financial corporations, and nonfinancial corporations. The only one of
the four segments that’s gone up in the last decade is government debt.

In relative terms, the transformation is even bigger if we look back to 2007, before
the Global Financial Crisis kicked off. Back then, government debt was the smallest
of the four categories, but because of the GFC and the pandemic, it’s now become
the biggest of the four categories.
That in turn has led to separate issues, with government bond yields at multi-year
highs, alongside rising net interest spending. So the fiscal space has become
limited. However, public balance sheets don’t face the same kind of system-wide

run that a bank would.

Overall, the post-2008 experience offers three key forward-looking lessons. First,
stronger bank balance sheets and the shift towards non-bank finance suggest

future stress may originate outside banks. As such, historical analyses centred on
banking crises may provide a less complete guide. Second, policymakers have
become increasingly effective at containing liquidity pressures and amplification.
And third, the shift in debt towards sovereign balance sheets has cushioned private-
sector shocks but narrowed the capacity for future intervention.
Section 4: What are potential candidates for the next crisis?

This section considers some of the current risks in the financial system and whether
they could be sowing the seeds for the next financial crisis. This section is not
designed to be a comprehensive list of financial risks, but rather an assessment of
three important areas where risks have risen in recent years.
1. Could AI spark broader vulnerabilities?
A huge driver of the current equity bull run has been the growth in AI-related stocks.
This report won’t explore the debate as to whether this is a bubble, but examine

whether a correction could eventually propagate into a systemic financial crisis. As
noted earlier, a reversal in the investment cycle need not cause a crisis in itself, and
the bursting of the dot com bubble also did not lead to a broader financial crisis.
Nevertheless, there are issues that could spark vulnerabilities. First, the rapid rise
in AI capex raises questions about whether the investment will pay off. The AI build-
out is on course to exceed the scale of past technological investment cycles, which

have often involved overinvestment even when the technologies ultimately proved
transformative. Second, funding is shifting from predominantly operating cash
flow towards external capital, and rising debt issuance in particular. Third, greater
equity-market concentration increases exposure to a reassessment of technology
valuations. Together, these developments make a correction a clear risk.
To assess the potential for systemic stress, we apply the framework in Greenwood,

Hanson, Shleifer and Sørensen (GHSS). This associates heightened crisis risk with
the combination of a strong run-up in asset prices and excessive credit growth,
estimating a 45% probability of a crisis within 3 years if the two thresholds in Figure
11 are met. On a backward-looking basis as of 2025, an economy-wide assessment
finds that the equity-price increase meets the framework’s “red zone” test, but
business credit growth has been considerably more subdued, given the slowdown

### Page 11

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 11
in credit following the 2022-23 tightening cycle. That is, going into 2026, the pattern
was one of a displacement of credit from other interest-sensitive sectors towards
AI and technology rather than any broad-based rise in leverage.
Nevertheless, US data does show a clear acceleration in credit trends so far in 2026.
To make a more forward-looking assessment, we incorporate the rise in issuance

in 2026 and indicatively extend it into 2027 using the estimated rise in AI capex
plans, assuming credit growth elsewhere in the US economy remains stable
(footnote: We see two-sided risks to this – we may understate the broader credit
demand in tech adjacent areas; conversely, this may understate the drag of higher
rates on credit demand in non-AI related sectors). We also extend the estimated
2027 trend into 2028, including an assumption that equity prices continue their

trend over the past 12 months, to provide an illustrative scenario over the next two
years. Under these assumptions, we would see a visible rise in aggregate US
business sector leverage, leaving the sector just shy of economy-wide red-zone
levels by 2028. As such, approaching that territory by 2028 is conceivable if the AI
cycle continues to accelerate, both in terms of increased debt borrowing and a
renewed rise in valuations.
While the GHSS framework gives a useful quantification of the potential risks, its

indicators also warrant some qualification, which we judge to come in opposing
directions:
n On the one hand, there is a risk that aggregate credit measures may
understate vulnerabilities when borrowing is highly concentrated. Circular
financing, leases and off-balance-sheet structures complicate the picture,
and while we incorporate the recent rise in payables in our leverage metric
to capture some of this risk, it may well be underestimated. Moreover,

realised interest costs remain below current market borrowing rates after
the lengthy period of ultra-low rates, so leverage trends alone may miss the
pressure from rising debt-service costs.
n On the other hand, the price-based metric used by GHSS to assess excess
asset price growth (the 3-year real rise in equity prices) may overstate
valuation excesses given the strong rise in earnings. Indeed, P/E multiples
have already seen a sizeable correction since late 2025, which was most

pronounced for software stocks, and currently there is little evidence of
overly stretched tech valuations, especially when compared to the dot com
boom.
The risk of stress amplification is also likely to depend on the nature of any AI
correction. A downgrading of the AI capex cycle due to slower growth in AI
demand, financing constraints or supply bottlenecks may result in weaker
valuations, leading to negative wealth effects and slower capital expenditure.

However, while this would create an economic headwind, it need not produce a
systemic crisis. And while parallels to the dot com bubble may well be relevant,
currently there are factors that suggest downside risks would be more moderate.
For instance, operating cash flow still plays an important role in financing the AI
buildout, whilst there has been a limited run-up in borrowing and much less
stretched valuations relative to the late-1990s.
Our assessment therefore remains that an AI correction is a clearer risk than an AI-

led systemic crisis at this point. For these risks to become more material, it would
need leverage to keep building but AI returns to eventually disappoint, raising the
likelihood of credit events and amplifying the investment slowdown through the
real economy. We will focus on this interaction between debt-service pressures and

### Page 12

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 12 Deutsche Bank AG
the role of credit events more in the next section.

2. Risks from higher rates and private credit
In the previous section we noted that modest economy-wide debt growth doesn’t
necessarily imply modest credit risk when debt-service costs are rising. As existing
debt reprices or is refinanced, borrowers can face greater pressure even without
additional borrowing. This is a key feature of the debt cycle, whereby vulnerabilities
accumulate during the expansion, and rising rates (often amid a monetary

tightening due to an overheating economy) can then expose them. These risks are
relevant in the context of the current rate hiking cycle, especially as much of the
catch up in debt service costs is yet to play out (see chart below).
With banking-sector risks having moderated since the GFC, the rapid expansion of
private credit and its opaque structure have understandably drawn attention as a
possible source of the next crisis. Parallels with the subprime credit boom have

been periodically made. However, we think these should not be overstated. Even
though the dollar size of the US private credit market is similar to that of subprime
around 2006-07, the market must be assessed relative to the economy (which has
doubled in nominal terms). Moreover, the slower build-up, restrictions on
redemptions and lower risks of direct wealth effects all suggest much more
moderate amplification risks than in 2008.
There are already signs that the private-credit growth cycle is slowing (see chart)

and our credit strategists have noted warning signs including weaker new inflows
into BDCs, increasing payment-in-kind interest and prospective refinancing
pressures. That said, defaults remain limited thus far so these should not be seen
as evidence of an imminent systemic event.

### Page 14

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 14 Deutsche Bank AG
Whether more systemic risks could emerge will depend on issues of solvency,
opacity and capacity for intervention.
n First, while redemption gates can limit immediate cash outflows and
reduce the need to sell illiquid loans under pressure, they do not restore a
borrower’s ability to service debt or absorb underlying credit losses. This
distinction between liquidity protection and solvency is therefore central to

judging whether a slowing credit cycle remains orderly.
n Opacity remains a concern, both in financing structures and in the
distribution of exposures among end-holders, including insurers and other
non-banks. This can make losses harder to identify and potential
amplification harder to assess.
n Policymakers’ success in containing liquidity pressures may not translate
into an effective response to credit events - liquidity provision alone cannot

repair impaired balance sheets. Extending support to opaque non-bank
exposures raises difficult questions about eligibility, loss allocation and the
threshold for intervention.
Quite aside from private credit, the fact that we are back in a rate hiking cycle from
the major central banks also adds to the current risks. Indeed, higher rates bring
down inflation, in part because they tighten financial conditions. But a
consequence of this is that it often reveals vulnerabilities in the financial system,

and makes a crisis more likely. For instance, it was a contributing factor behind the
2023 regional banking crisis, as banks had growing unrealised losses on their
securities portfolio when rates began to rise again. So even if higher rates don’t
directly cause a crisis, they can often be a key contributor. Indeed, hiking cycles
have regularly coincided with some kind of shock, as the experience of recent
decades demonstrates.

3. Stretched fiscal positions and possible NBFI amplifiers
As discussed earlier, the migration of debt towards sovereign balance sheets has

helped cushion private-sector shocks, but this has come at the cost of higher fiscal
risks. Higher public debt and sovereign yields have limited the room for fiscal
stimulus, while persistent inflation has limited the room for monetary stimulus. This
makes another round of coordinated macroeconomic support harder to deliver.
The issue with high public debt is that it if fiscal sustainability is questioned, that can
raise sovereign borrowing costs, which can amplify existing private-sector

vulnerabilities via a tightening in financial conditions and slower growth.
The financial risk posed by sovereign stress will also depend on the financial
amplification channels. These were clear in the European sovereign crisis with the
bank-sovereign loop, but the changing structure of sovereign markets suggests
that the sovereign–NBFI feedback loop may now warrant increasing attention. This
was visible with the 2022 LDI crisis in the UK, while a more recent concern is the run

up in leveraged hedge fund positions in sovereign bond markets, with hedge funds’
sovereign bond exposures more than doubling since 2022 (see chart below). As
noted also by the IMF, this reflects greater issuance and a rising share of sovereign
debt held by hedge funds.
The lessons from Black Monday, LTCM and the LDI crisis offer reassurance that
intervention can contain market stress originating outside the banking system. But
they are not a guarantee that future liquidity shocks will remain economically

benign. The greater scale and interconnectedness of non-bank finance mean that
we can’t simply rely on historical examples today.
Looking forward, a combination of sovereign repricing, leveraged financial
amplification and a more constrained policy response risks proving harder to
resolve than past episodes.

Conclusion
Whilst a major financial crisis has been avoided since the GFC and the subsequent
sovereign crisis, the problem of financial instability has yet to be permanently

solved. On the one hand, reforms to the financial system and the swift actions of
policymakers have helped to prevent a repeat. But as we know from Hyman
Minsky’s warning, lengthy periods of stability can often lay the foundations for the
next crisis, even if these take place slowly. Indeed, risks often shift rather than
disappear.
Looking forward, there are several vectors of risk worth paying attention to. But
financial crises often start in areas where people are paying little attention, or risks

are assumed to be contained. Indeed, it is the areas where risk is underappreciated
which often prove the most vulnerable.
As such, predicting where the next crisis might arise is inherently difficult. But the
topic is a vital one, given the long-lasting damage that financial crises can cause
when they do occur. Indeed, with the fiscal space for sovereigns increasingly
constrained today, the margin for a future policy error is limited.

《歐洲信用債市受法國財政疑慮波及》
歐洲信用市場雖受AI相關債券波動影響較小，但因
能源價格高企，加上近期法國主權和財政風險加劇，
歐洲信用債市十月份表現也有不小波動，尤其是非
投資級債信用利差一度擴大逾60個基點。歐洲銀行
債是各產業信用債主權風險曝險較高的類別，因歐
洲銀行多數不及美國銀行營運遍及全球且業務多元，
此外也持有不少主權債資產，十月份市場表現也反
映此一狀況，主權債的波動更加重了歐洲銀行債的
跌幅，法國銀行債利差擴大幅度最為明顯，所幸法
國主要銀行包括法國巴黎銀行以及興業銀行3Q26獲
利狀況良好，即使經營環境惡化，從不良貸款率與

信用風險成本等資產品質指標來看法國巴黎銀行持
平前季、法國興業銀行僅微幅上升。
目前市場關注的焦點仍是法國財政整頓的路徑，法
國目前的債務負擔約佔GDP的118%至119%，高盛
預計，如果沒有更有效的財政調整，到2030年初負
債比率將進一步上升至接近125%，如要穩定債務
佔GDP的比重，法國需要實現約佔GDP 1%的財政
盈餘，但目前的財政赤字約佔2.5% GDP。法國政
府當局可能在年底前通過一項撙節的預算案，若沒
通過法國主權債與信用債可能進一步惡化，但即使
通過法國主權債利率要大幅回落的機會仍不大，可
能要到2027年法國大選後主權債才有機會趨於穩定，
屆時法國銀行債或有布局機會。

《非投資級債美國仍優於歐洲》
儘管目前不論是美國還是歐洲的非投資級債在高利
率持續下信用基本面都已出現分化，如CCC等級的
償債能力指標顯著疲弱，但衡量整體債信風險仍是
歐洲較美國為高；原因包括歐洲的債務到期牆較美
國明顯，且美國非投資級債2028年前到期的債券分
佈在相對廣泛的行業，而當前的賤售債券主要集中
在媒體、原物料與電信、非必需消費等特定領域，
上述較少重疊降低落入賤售債券利差區間的發行人
同時面臨債務到期後償債或再融資令違約風險增加
的可能。另一方面，歐洲非投資級債的到期牆更為
陡峭，且市場利率與2028年前到期債券的票面利率

差距大於美非投資級債，反映再融資壓力更鉅，尤
其是歐洲CCC等級。
分析歐洲非投資級債各產業債務到期狀況可發現
2028年前到期債務產業相對集中，且易受景氣影響
的非必需消費在到期債務與賤售債券都佔有一定比
例，另外槓桿率本就較高的電信業佔2028年前到期
債務9%，融資成本大幅上漲恐令這些發行人債信體
質進一步惡化。

## Document 466: 【粤开宏观】中央经济工作会议前瞻

宏观点评 
 【粤开宏观】中央经济工作会议前瞻 
2026 年 11 月 18 日  投资要点

近期报告

一、中央经济工作会议对 2027 年经济工作预计有哪些重点部署？ 
预计 2027 年经济工作将继续坚持稳中求进的总基调，把巩固经济回升向好基
础、增强经济内生增长动力作为重要着力点。在稳增长方面，会议可能继续
释放加强逆周期和跨周期调节、保持宏观政策支持力度的积极信号，更加注
重政策的连续性、针对性和协同性。 
二、可能有哪些进一步的财政和货币政策措施？ 
预计 2027 年将继续实施更加积极的财政政策和适度宽松的货币政策。财政方
面，预计赤字率将继续保持在 4%左右的较高水平，合理扩大超长期特别国债
和地方政府专项债券支持力度，财政支出将更加注重提振消费、保障民生和
扩大有效投资。货币方面，仍有适时降准降息的空间，同时进一步发挥结构
性货币政策工具作用，加强对民营企业、科技创新、消费和重点领域投资的
支持。 
三、可能有哪些进一步支持房地产市场的政策举措？ 
下一步应更加重视稳定住房需求和改善市场预期，继续因城施策优化限制性
措施，降低合理购房成本，加大对刚性和改善性住房需求的支持。可以根据
政策实施效果和不同城市房地产市场情况，研究延续和优化现有购房贷款贴
息政策，进一步降低合理住房需求的购房成本，同时完善房地产项目融资支
持机制，稳妥推进存量住房盘活和城市更新。 
四、可能有哪些进一步提振居民消费、稳定投资的措施？ 
消费方面，应在继续发挥短期促消费政策作用的同时，更加注重提高居民消
费能力、改善消费预期，把稳就业、促增收放在更加突出的位置，特别是提
高中低收入群体的消费能力，完善养老、医疗、托育等社会保障和公共服务
体系，减轻居民长期支出顾虑。投资方面，应加快重大项目落地和资金拨付，
强化政府投资对社会投资的带动作用，通过改善营商环境、清理拖欠企业账
款、拓宽民间资本投资渠道等方式，稳定企业预期，激发民间投资活力。地
方政府债务风险化解不仅关系财政金融安全，也是恢复地方财政支出能力、
稳定经济增长的重要条件。 
五、财政、货币、房地产和消费政策应如何协同发力？ 
2027 年宏观政策应更加注重财政、货币、房地产和消费政策的系统协同。财
政政策应保持必要的支出力度，更加注重改善居民收入、完善公共服务和带
动有效投资；货币政策着力降低融资成本、畅通信用传导，为实体经济提供
适宜的金融环境；房地产政策重点稳定市场预期、促进供需平衡，缓解房地
产调整对居民消费和地方财政的影响；消费政策则应更加注重提高居民收入
和完善社会保障。通过各项政策协调配合，推动居民消费恢复、企业盈利改

### Page 2

宏观点评
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  2 / 7
善和投资信心增强，逐步形成需求扩张与经济增长相互促进的良性循环。
风险提示：宏观政策超预期、外部环境超预期

### Page 4

宏观点评
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  4 / 7
一、中央经济工作会议对 2027 年经济工作预计有哪些重点部
署？
预计 2027 年经济工作将继续坚持稳中求进的总基调，把巩固经济回升向好基础、增
强经济内生增长动力作为重要着力点。具体来看，需求端将更加突出提振居民消费和扩
大有效投资，供给端将着力推动科技创新和产业升级，改革方面将进一步优化营商环境、
激发民营经济活力，同时统筹做好房地产、地方政府债务等重点领域风险防范化解工作，
推动经济实现质的有效提升和量的合理增长。
在稳增长方面，会议可能继续释放加强逆周期和跨周期调节、保持宏观政策支持力
度的积极信号，更加注重政策的连续性、针对性和协同性。同时，宏观政策将更加重视
短期稳增长与中长期增强内生动力相结合，通过改善居民收入预期、增强企业投资信心
和深化重点领域改革，促进消费与投资良性互动，推动经济持续回升向好。
二、可能有哪些进一步的财政和货币政策措施？
预计 2027 年将继续实施更加积极的财政政策和适度宽松的货币政策。财政方面，预

计赤字率将继续保持在 4%左右的较高水平，合理扩大超长期特别国债和地方政府专项债
券支持力度，财政支出将更加注重提振消费、保障民生和扩大有效投资。货币方面，仍
有适时降准降息的空间，同时进一步发挥结构性货币政策工具作用，加强对民营企业、
科技创新、消费和重点领域投资的支持。
总体来看，财政政策仍有一定发力空间，尤其是中央财政加杠杆的空间相对更大，
但地方财政受到债务负担和收入增长放缓的约束。货币政策也具备进一步宽松的条件，
但需要统筹考虑银行净息差、汇率稳定和有效融资需求不足等因素。下一步应更加注重
财政与货币政策的协调配合，财政政策侧重扩大有效需求，货币政策着力营造适宜的融
资和流动性环境，并通过财政贴息、融资担保、风险补偿和政策性金融工具等方式，提
高政策传导效率，推动资金供给更好地转化为消费需求和有效投资。
三、可能有哪些进一步支持房地产市场的政策举措？
房地产不仅关系开发投资和上下游产业链，也通过财富效应、地方土地出让收入等
渠道影响消费与投资。当前房地产市场仍处于深度调整阶段，稳定房地产市场有助于缓

解居民资产负债表调整压力、改善市场预期，减轻房地产调整对宏观经济的持续拖累。
政策方面，下一步应更加重视稳定住房需求和改善市场预期，继续因城施策优化限
制性措施，降低合理购房成本，加大对刚性和改善性住房需求的支持。可以根据政策实
施效果和不同城市房地产市场情况，研究延续和优化现有购房贷款贴息政策，进一步降
低合理住房需求的购房成本，同时完善房地产项目融资支持机制，稳妥推进存量住房盘
活和城市更新。预计会议将继续强调稳定房地产市场、加快构建房地产发展新模式，通
过支持合理住房需求、改善市场供需关系和化解存量风险，促进房地产市场平稳健康发
展。
四、可能有哪些进一步提振居民消费、稳定投资的措施？
消费方面，应在继续发挥短期促消费政策作用的同时，更加注重提高居民消费能力、
改善消费预期，把稳就业、促增收放在更加突出的位置，特别是提高中低收入群体的消
费能力，完善养老、医疗、托育等社会保障和公共服务体系，减轻居民长期支出顾虑。

### Page 5

宏观点评
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  5 / 7
投资方面，应加快重大项目落地和资金拨付，强化政府投资对社会投资的带动作用，通
过改善营商环境、清理拖欠企业账款、拓宽民间资本投资渠道等方式，稳定企业预期，
激发民间投资活力。
地方政府债务风险化解不仅关系财政金融安全，也是恢复地方财政支出能力、稳定
经济增长的重要条件。一方面，应继续推进存量债务置换，优化债务期限和成本结构，
减轻地方偿债压力；另一方面，适度提高中央财政对地方的支持力度，增强基层财政保
障能力，为地方正常履职和必要投资创造条件。同时，严控新增隐性债务，推动地方融
资平台分类转型。提高政策效果的关键，是将化债与稳增长相结合，更加注重财政资金
使用效益，避免低效重复投资，增强财政支出对居民消费和社会投资的带动作用。
五、财政、货币、房地产和消费政策应如何协同发力？
2027 年宏观政策应更加注重财政、货币、房地产和消费政策的系统协同。财政政策
应保持必要的支出力度，更加注重改善居民收入、完善公共服务和带动有效投资；货币

政策着力降低融资成本、畅通信用传导，为实体经济提供适宜的金融环境；房地产政策
重点稳定市场预期、促进供需平衡，缓解房地产调整对居民消费和地方财政的影响；消
费政策则应更加注重提高居民收入和完善社会保障。通过各项政策协调配合，推动居民
消费恢复、企业盈利改善和投资信心增强，逐步形成需求扩张与经济增长相互促进的良
性循环。
当前最大的挑战，在于如何将宏观政策支持真正转化为居民消费和企业投资的内生
动力。居民收入和资产预期偏弱、企业盈利能力和投资回报预期不足，以及部分地方财
政约束较强，都可能影响政策传导效果。因此，2027 年不仅需要保持必要的宏观政策力
度，更要通过深化改革、改善收入分配、稳定房地产市场和优化营商环境，增强居民消
费能力与意愿，改善企业盈利和投资预期，推动政策支持与内生动力相互促进，为经济
持续回升向好提供更加坚实的支撑。

股票投资评级说明 
投资评级分为股票投资评级和行业投资评级。

股票投资评级标准 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内公司股价的涨跌幅度相对同期沪深 300 指数的涨跌幅为基准，投资建议的评级标准为： 
买入：相对大盘涨幅大于 10%； 
增持：相对大盘涨幅在 5%~10%之间； 
持有：相对大盘涨幅在-5%~5%之间； 
减持：相对大盘涨幅小于-5%。

行业投资评级标准 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内行业股票指数的涨跌幅度相对同期沪深 300 指数的涨跌幅为基准，投资建议的评级标准为： 
增持：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报高于基准指数 5%以上； 
中性：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报介于基准指数-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报低于基准指数 5%以下。

台北富邦銀行

債券市場總評：投等債吸引力增
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎通膨壓力續高，年底升息預期仍存 
10 月上旬以來，美債殖利率自高檔回落，高殖利率逐
步吸引買盤，股市震盪亦帶動避險資金回流債市。不
過，受燃料與能源價格上揚帶動，9 月 CPI 年增率由
3.4%升至 3.7%，PPI 年增率亦由 5.6%升至 6.3%，核
心 CPI 與核心 PPI 年增幅也高於預期，顯示物價壓力
仍高。加上零售銷售月增 0.8%優於預期、初領失業金
人數維持低位，反映消費與勞動市場仍具韌性，市場
因此雖傾向 Fed 本月先按兵不動，但 12 月再升息 1
碼的預期仍存。 
圖 1  9 月 CPI、PPI 年增率同步走高

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/19 
▎避險買盤增溫，殖利率回落、債市修復 
近期美債買盤回升也反映在公債標售結果，3 年、10
年及 30 年期標售需求皆表現穩健或強勁，提供長端美
債支撐。雖然中東情勢反覆使油價維持高檔，通膨預
期仍偏高，但 Fed 官員多傾向先觀察，緩和短期升息
壓力。此外，高殖利率環境使 AI 與科技股估值面臨修
正壓力，股市震盪帶動避險資金流入美債，使 10 年期
美債殖利率自 10 月初 5.30%高位顯著回降至低點
5.04%後反彈。

圖 2 公債與投等債反彈較強，惟油價仍處高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/19 
◼ 9 月 CPI、PPI 升溫，消費與就業具韌性，年底升息預期仍存。不過，美債標售穩
健、Fed 官員偏向不急於在 10 月升息，股市震盪帶動避險買盤，10 年期殖利率
回降後反彈，債市自低檔修復，近期美國公債與投等債反彈幅度高於非投等債。 
◼ 近期美國投等債利差反彈相對受控，Q4「淨淨」發行量可望逐月下降，年底季節
性亦有利，技術面改善，企業獲利強勁，信用面穩健。美投等債殖利率仍處高檔，
高票息提供報酬緩衝，歷史經驗顯示，高殖利率通常伴隨較佳未來 12 個月報酬。 
◼ 產業配置上，金融債存續期間較短、利率敏感度較低，加上銀行貸款與存款維持
成長，資產品質穩健，防禦性相對較佳。科技債殖利率與 OAS 高於整體投等債市
場，反映 AI 資本支出擴張推升發債供給，但科技企業財務體質仍佳，利差走寬後
收益補償提升。整體投資可偏向中短天期，依風險承受度與收益需求擇優布局。

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
隨長端殖利率回落，10 月以來主要債券自低點反彈，
其中美國公債與投等債對利率回落較為敏感，反彈力道
高於受股市波動影響較大的非投等債。

▎信用利差走寬，美投等債相對具韌性 
近期債市明顯承壓，全球公債殖利率一度快速上行、利
率波動升高，加上企業債供給壓力偏高，信用利差也隨
之走寬。儘管近期美國投等債與非投等債 OAS 自低位
有所回升，但若以近 10 年歷史區間比較，目前仍處於
相對低檔。

圖 3 近 10 年美國投等債與非投等債 OAS 走勢

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/19

而在這波信用利差走寬過程中，美國投等債 OAS 升幅
明顯低於其他主要信用債市場，仍展現較佳防禦性。

圖 4 近期美國投等債 OAS 升幅相對受控

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/14

▎Q4 淨供給緩和，投等債利差有望收斂 
企業債發行量維持高檔，仍對債市形成一定壓力，但進
入 Q4 後，美國投等債市場的供給壓力可望逐步緩和。
若進一步觀察「淨淨」發行量（net-net issuance），
即扣除到期本金、贖回款與票息等回流資金後，市場實
際需消化的新增公司債供給，根據 J.P. Morgan 預估，
Q4「淨淨」發行量將逐月下降，新增供給對利差的壓力
可望減輕。

圖 5  Q4 美國投等債淨淨供給量預估逐月下降

資料來源：JPM，2026/10/2

同時，過去 4 年平均來看，美國投等債利差在 11 月、 
12 月偏向收斂，年底季節性有助支撐利差表現。供給
壓力下降與年底季節性相互配合，使第 4 季美國投等債
利差具備較有利的技術面條件。 
圖 6 近 4 年 11 月~12 月美國投等債利差呈現收窄

註：本圖採用 J.P. Morgan 美國投等債指數(JULI)。負值代表月末利
差較月初收窄，正值代表利差擴大。 
資料來源：JPM，2026/10/2

### Page 3

3

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎企業獲利預估強勁，支撐信用基本面 
近期殖利率上行雖受到通膨與貨幣政策預期影響，部分
也反映美國經濟與企業獲利仍具韌性。AI 基礎建設需求
與大型科技企業資本支出持續擴張，加上企業利潤率維
持高檔，成為獲利成長的重要支撐。標普 500 企業獲利
自 2026 年以來明顯加速，第 1 季與第 2 季分別年成長
29%與 52%，Q3 與 Q4 預估仍達 30%與 28%。獲利
動能延續，有助維持投等債發行公司的償債能力與財務
彈性，支撐高利率環境下的信用韌性。

圖 7 標普 500 企業 Q3、Q4 獲利年增率將維持高成長

資料來源：JPM，2026/10/2

▎高殖利率提供報酬緩衝 
目前美國投等債殖利率仍處相對高檔，較高的殖利率可
提高票息緩衝，也為後續報酬提供較有利的起點。根據
JPM 自 1990 年以來的歷史資料顯示，美國投等債當期
殖利率與未來 12 個月總報酬呈正向關係，殖利率較高
時，後續 12 個月報酬通常也較佳。在利率仍可能波動
的環境下，較高票息有助吸收部分價格波動，使目前的
收益條件較過去低殖利率時期更具支撐。 
圖 8 高殖利率通常伴隨較佳 12 個月報酬

資料來源：JPM，2026/10/12 
▎金融偏防禦，科技債補償提升 
綜合信用基本面、供需條件與收益水準，美國投等債整
體展望仍偏正向。在產業方面，金融債在殖利率接近整
體投等債市場的情況下，存續期間明顯較短。近期大型
銀行財報亦顯示，貸款與存款維持成長，整體資產品質
仍穩健，為金融債基本面提供支撐。科技債殖利率與
OAS 則高於整體市場，主要反映 AI 資本支出擴張帶動
發債供給快速增加。儘管融資需求升高，大型科技企業
整體資產負債表仍相對穩健，利差走寬後的收益補償也
有所提升。整體配置上，在利率仍具不確定性的環境下，
可偏向中短天期，並依風險承受度與收益需求擇優布局。

圖 9 美國投等債各產業殖利率與存續期間

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/15

圖 10 美國投等債各產業 OAS 與存續期間

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/15

## Document 529: 債券市場總評：製藥債穩中轉強

Source PDF: all report pdf/債券市場總評：製藥債穩中轉強.pdf
Report source: Fubon
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1 
*上述相關標的僅舉例說明，非單一標的推薦。 
台北富邦銀行 
Nov 13，2026 | 3:00 PM (GMT+8) 
債券市場總評：製藥債穩中轉強
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎就業與通膨具韌性，升息預期升溫 
自 11 月以來，美債殖利率先降後升。期中選舉後，民
主黨拿下國會兩院，財政擴張可能受到更多制衡，加
上科技股大跌與美伊情勢一度降溫，帶動避險需求升
高、長端殖利率回落。不過，10 月非農新增 16.5 萬
人、失業率降至 4.1%，CPI 與核心 CPI 月增率分別為
0.4%與 0.3%，雙雙高於預期，加上多位 Fed 官員言
論轉趨鷹派，推升 12 月升息預期機率至 100%。

圖 1  10 月非農優於預期，CPI 月增偏高

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/12 
▎中東情勢反覆，油價回升壓抑債市 
近日川普表示美國無須與伊朗達成協議，使市場對美
伊情勢持續降溫的期待減弱，國際油價再度反彈，通
膨疑慮隨之升高。加上市場對後續升息的預期轉強，
10 年期美債殖利率由 11 月初低點 4.83%快速反彈至
5.13%，重新升破 5%。在地緣政治與利率風險反覆
下，市場波動仍高，隨殖利率快速回升，近期債券價
格再度回落，債市再度轉趨承壓。

圖 2 殖利率隨油價反彈，美國公債、投等債同步回落

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/12 
◼ 期中選舉民主黨贏得國會兩院，政策制衡預期升高，油價回落、避險需求升溫，
殖利率走低。不過，10 月非農與 CPI 高於預期，Fed 官員偏鷹，12 月升息預期
升溫。中東情勢反覆、油價回升，10 年期殖利率反彈至 5.13%，債市轉趨承壓。 
◼ 近期製藥債利差彈幅低於整體投等債，防禦性較佳。醫療債升評潛力中，近 70%
集中製藥業，3Q 醫療 EPS 超預期幅度居 11 大產業之首。2027 年製藥次產業 EPS
預估年增 24.2%，高於醫療保健與 S&P500 整體，製藥業獲利信用趨勢同步轉強。 
◼ 3Q 多數大型藥廠營運優於預期，現金流充足，槓桿維持健康水準。禮來*與嬌生
*財務與營運穩健，諾和諾德*具成長動能。默克*現金流與槓桿改善，但仍有產品
集中風險。輝瑞*與必治妥施貴寶*則面臨較大專利到期與產品接替壓力。在獲利
與財務條件支撐下，大型製藥債仍具配置價值。

2026 年初至今報酬率 
美國非投等債報酬率 0.67% 
美國公債報酬率 -1.47% 
美國投等債報酬率 -1.78%

美國 10 年公債殖利率 5.13%

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎製藥債利差展現韌性，升評潛力突出 
近期美債殖利率回升、整體債市重新承壓，但信用市場
內部的產業表現仍明顯分化。4Q 以來，美國投資等級
債 OAS 約擴大 7.6bps，相較之下，醫療保健製藥僅擴
大約 2.1bps，醫療保健整體亦僅約 3.5bps，與必需消
費、公用事業等防禦性產業相近，並明顯低於科技與通
訊產業的利差擴張幅度。在信用市場波動升高的環境下，
製藥債利差表現仍展現相對較佳的防禦性。

圖 3 近期製藥等防禦性產業 OAS 彈幅相對小

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/10

製藥債相對穩健的利差表現，也有信用評級趨勢支撐。
目前醫療保健投等債中，約有 18%具升評潛力，規模約
2,280 億美元，其中製藥業占近 70%，為信用評級改善
潛力最集中的領域。過去數年部分藥廠因併購提高槓桿，
隨著後續債務削減與資產負債表改善，信用指標逐步修
復，評級上調空間也隨之增加。整體而言，製藥債近期
兼具相對穩定的利差表現與信用改善潛力，基本面在醫
療保健產業中相對突出。 
圖 4 醫療升評潛力債務規模中，製藥債占近 7 成

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/9 
▎3Q 醫療獲利超預期，製藥成長將加速 
醫療保健近期獲利表現強勁。2026 年第 3 季醫療保健
EPS 高於市場預期幅度約 14%，居 S&P500 11 大產業
之首，亦明顯較 S&P500 的 4%高。公用事業、能源與
工業等產業雖同樣優於預期，但幅度仍低於醫療保健，
反映近期醫療企業獲利表現具有相對優勢。製藥業第 3 
季營運亦維持強勁，多家公司營收優於預期並上調財測，
加上新藥逐步接替成熟產品線、藥價政策風險減退，進
一步支撐大型藥廠獲利展望。 
圖 5 3Q 醫療保健獲利超預期幅度居 11 大產業之冠

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/10

彭博預估，2027 年 S&P500 企業 EPS 將年增 18.7%，
其中醫療保健產業預估年增約 19.8%，在 11 大產業中
僅次於資訊技術。進一步來看，製藥次產業 EPS 預估年
增約 24.2%，且較 2026 年大幅回升，成長改善幅度最
為突出。在多數產業成長動能放緩的情況下，製藥業獲
利表現相對強勁。 
圖 6 2027 年製藥次產業獲利預期成長動能強勁

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/10

標普 500 企業: +4%

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎大型藥廠現金流穩，多數維持低槓桿 
大型製藥公司具備穩健的現金流基礎。2026 年主要藥
廠預估自由現金流普遍具一定規模，其中禮來*與嬌生*
分別約 237 億與 224 億美元，輝瑞*、默克*與必治妥
施貴寶*亦約達 132～134 億美元。大型藥廠在維持一
定營運規模的同時，仍能產生可觀自由現金流，提供債
務償還與資本運用所需的財務彈性。

圖 7  主要藥廠 2026 年預估營收、自由現金流量

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/10 
*上述相關標的僅舉例說明，非單一標的推薦。

從獲利能力與槓桿來看，諾和諾德*與禮來*EBITDA 利
潤率分別約 49%與 46%，淨槓桿約 0.7x 與 1.0x，嬌生
*淨槓桿亦約 0.8x。相較之下，輝瑞*與默克*淨槓桿約
達 2.7x 與 2.5x。整體來看，多數大型藥廠淨槓桿仍維
持低至中度水準。

圖 8 主要藥廠 EBITDA 利潤率與淨槓桿

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/10 
*上述相關標的僅舉例說明，非單一標的推薦。 
進一步觀察財務比率，嬌生*、禮來*與諾和諾德*的自由
現金流對債務比率均超過 50%，必治妥施貴寶*約 48%，
現金流覆債能力相對較佳。相較之下，默克*約 33%，
輝瑞*約 22%，現金流對債務的覆蓋程度相對較低。

表 1 主要製藥大廠的財務比率

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11

▎醫療防禦性佳，製藥信用轉強 
近期市場資金轉向防禦性產業，醫療保健受惠於獲利
表現改善及政策風險減輕，產業表現明顯轉強，其中
製藥業的營運與信用趨勢相對突出。 
大型藥廠中，禮來*受惠於肥胖症與糖尿病業務快速成
長，現金流持續增加且槓桿預期進一步下降。嬌生*則
由製藥業務穩健成長及較低槓桿支撐，財務體質相對
穩健。諾和諾德*仍具有肥胖症業務成長動能，但面臨
價格與競爭壓力。默克*現金流與槓桿趨勢改善，近期
研發進展亦有助擴充產品線，但核心產品營收占比仍
高，產品集中風險尚未完全消除。輝瑞*與必治妥施貴
寶*近期營運雖優於預期，未來數年仍面臨較大的專利
到期與產品接替壓力。整體而言，製藥信用仍偏正
面，大型製藥債在獲利、現金流與財務結構支撐下，
仍具配置價值。

（Eli Lilly）
全球大型製藥公司，主要布

胖症與糖尿病業務維持強勁成長，獲利與現金流持續
擴張，槓桿預期進一步下降，信用評級仍有改善空
間。
諾和諾德*
（Novo Nordisk）
丹麥大型製藥公司，業務以
糖尿病與肥胖症治療為主。
3Q 表現優於預期並上調財測，肥胖症業務仍具成長
動能。不過美國市場仍面臨價格壓力與競爭升高，中
期成長動能承壓。
輝瑞*
（Pfizer）
全球大型製藥公司，產品涵
蓋腫瘤、疫苗、內科與罕見
疾病等領域。

改善，但未來數年仍面臨較大的專利到期壓力，新產
品接替與降低槓桿仍是主要課題。
默克*
（Merck）
美國大型製藥公司，以腫

為主。
3Q 營收與 EPS 優於預期，近期研發進展亦有改善。
現金流與槓桿趨勢改善，產品線逐步擴充，但核心產
品營收占比較高，產品集中風險仍在。
必治妥施貴寶*
（Bristol Myers
Squibb）
美國大型製藥公司，主要布

嬌生*

整體財務體質相對穩健。

## Document 543: 内外経済ウォッチ『アジア・新興国～台湾､AI･半導体特需で高成長も､金融市場に懸念材料～』(2026年12月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/内外経済ウォッチ『アジア・新興国～台湾､AI･半導体特需で高成長も､金融市場に懸念材料～』(2026年12月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

アジア・新興国
～台湾､AI･半導体特需で高成長も､金融市場に懸念材料～
台湾では、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大が景気
の追い風となる動きが続く。台湾は、半導体の設計と製造
を水平分業し、製造受託に特化するファウンドリモデルを
確立しており、世界のファウンドリ市場の４分の３以上の
シェアを占めるとされる。さらに、半導体の後工程を担う
OSAT（半導体受託製造サービス）分野でも、世界シェア
の半分近くを占める。
背景には、新竹サイエンスパークなどを中心に、設計・
製造・装置・材料・検査の企業が地理的に集積しており、試
作から量産までのフィードバックや改良を短期間で進めら
れるサプライチェーンが構築されていることがある。こう
したなか、９月の輸出額は前年比＋60.9％と高い伸びをみ
せるとともに、先行きの輸出動向を示す輸出受注額も同＋
62.4％となり、ともに過去最高を更新するなど旺盛な動
きが続いている。
台湾経済は、ＧＤＰに占める輸出比率が８割弱にのぼり、
アジア太平洋地域のなかでも構造面で外需依存度が相

対的に高い。このため、外需の拡大は景気を大幅に押し
上げる要因となる。こうしたなか、７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長
率（速報値）は前年同期比＋14.36％となり、前期比年率
ベースの成長率も＋13.48％と二けたの高い伸びが続い
ていることが確認された。
世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大を反映して、財輸
出が大幅に押し上げられる動きがみられる。さらに、ＡＩ・半
導体関連需要の旺盛さを追い風に、半導体製造設備や
データセンター関連の投資も拡大しており、設備投資も
景気を押し上げる動きが確認されている。このため、中銀
は９月の定例会合において、2026年通年の経済成長率
が11.48％と1987年以来の高い伸びとなる見通しを示
すなど、足元の台湾景気は活況を呈している。
世界的なAI・半導体需要が台湾景気の追い風に
2026年の経済成長率は40年弱ぶりの高水準に
一方、台湾は近年、脱石炭・脱原発を図るべく、ＬＮＧの
輸入を拡大させてきた。しかし、イラン情勢の悪化による
国際価格の上昇や輸送の混乱などの影響が直撃してい

る。また、2025年にはすべての原発の稼働を停止する脱
原発を実現したが、エネルギーの安定供給を理由に再稼
働を検討するなど、政策の見直しを迫られるうえ、石炭火
力を予備電源として活用する動きもみられる。
背景には、半導体の安定的な生産・供給を維持するうえ
で、膨大な電力の確保が不可欠なことがある。エネル
ギー価格の上昇に加え、食料品やサービス価格などにも
上昇圧力が掛かるなか、９月のインフレ率は前年同月比＋
2.73％と2024年３月以来の高い伸びとなった。さらに、
景気の堅調さも追い風にコアインフレ率も加速するな
ど、インフレ圧力が強まっている。
中銀は９月の定例会合において、10会合連続で政策金
利を据え置いた。とはいえ、２会合連続で２名の政策委員
が利上げを主張するなど、政策運営を巡る意見が分かれ
る動きもみられる。
一方、世界的に金利上昇圧力が強まるなか、ＡＩ・半導体
関連需要の見方に不透明感が出ている。こうした見方を
反映して、台湾でも時価総額の大きい半導体関連企業を
中心に株価が調整している。さらに、通貨台湾ドル相場も

下落圧力を強めている。主要株価指数の調整幅は、韓国
などと比べて小幅にとどまるも、仮に市場の混乱が長期
化すれば、実体経済にも少なからず影響が及ぶ可能性が
ある。その場合、中銀は『タカ派』姿勢の修正を迫られる
ことも考えられる。一方、台湾ドル安による輸入物価の上
昇がインフレ圧力を増幅させるリスクもあるなど、政策運
営の舵取りは一層難しくなることが予想される。
エネルギーの安定供給に苦慮する展開が続く
中銀の政策の舵取りは一段と困難さが増す
経済調査部　主席エコノミスト　西濵 徹（にしはま とおる）
8第一ライフ研レポート 2026.12
内外経済ウォッチ

## Document 552: 凱基投顧_2357 華碩_余昀澄_20261112

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_2357 華碩_余昀澄_20261112.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

動態更新 筆電代工 ‧ 台灣

華碩 
(2357.TW/2357 TT)

獲利率亮眼；伺服器帶動 2027年營收成長 
重要訊息 
3Q26 EPS 達 29.13 元，優於凱基預估，因營利率優於預期。管理層表示
4Q26-2Q27 PC 需求將放緩，但 2027 年伺服器營收將強勁成長。 
評論及分析 
3Q26 獲利大幅優於預期。3Q26 EPS 29.13 元，優於凱基和市場預估
38%、29%，主因營利率優於預期。3Q26 品牌營收季增 7%，主因市占率
擴張使 PC 營收季增 19%，抵銷伺服器營收季減 11%之影響 (AI 專案遞延至
10 月)。品牌毛利率季減 0.6ppts 至 15.9%，但營利率季增 0.4ppts 至
8.5%，超前凱基預估，主因 PC 營收比重擴大使產品組合有利。 
4Q26 營收展望優於預期。華碩展望 4Q26 伺服器營收季增 100%，大幅優
於凱基預估，主因 AI 伺服器需求強勁，且部份專案由 9 月遞延至 4Q26。管
理層預期 4Q26 PC 營收季減 15%，符合季節性和凱基預期；零組件營收則
預期季減 10%。因伺服器展望優於預期，我們上修 4Q26 品牌營收至季增
21%。考量 PC 需求放緩與 AI 伺服器貢獻增加不利產品組合，凱基預估
4Q26 營利率季減至 5.8%。但考量營收預估上修，我們調升 4Q26 EPS 至
21.7 元，季減 26%，2026 年 EPS 亦上調至 89.66 元，年增 50%。  
2027 年伺服器營收強勁成長，但 PC 需求放緩。管理層展望 2027 年伺服器
營收年增高雙位數。華碩表示客戶 2027 年資料中心興建計畫目前無異動，
而公司持續分散客群，增加 neocloud、二三線 CSP、AI 實驗室與主權 AI 專
案以降低客戶推遲出貨的風險。公司 Neocloud 客戶 40-50%來自亞洲，歐洲
30%、美國 20-30%，而華碩持續新增歐美客戶。然而，公司預期 4Q26-
2Q27 PC 需求放緩，2027 年全球 PC 需求年減 10-15%，但目標華碩出貨優
於產業平均。因市場需求降溫，公司可能較難將零組件漲價完全轉嫁至系統
零售價，營利率恐承壓。我們下修 2027 年 PC 營收至年減 4%，但調升伺服
器營收至 5,760 億元，年增 54% (營收比重 48%)。儘管營收預估調升至年
增 19%，但考慮利息支出較高，維持 2027 年 EPS 82.51 元，年減 8%。 
投資建議 
我們將評價基準改為 2027 年獲利，上修目標價至 1,155 元，基於 14 倍
2027 年 EPS 預估 (前次為 14x 2026-27F EPS)，評等維持「增加持股」。 
投資風險 
筆電需求疲弱；AI GPU 供給緊缺；匯率波動。 
增加持股‧維持 
  收盤價 November 12 (NT$)  890 
12 個月目標價 (NT$) 1,155 
前次目標價 (NT$) 1,130

2. 伺服器帶動 4Q26 營收展望優於預期。 
3. 伺服器將是 2027 年的營收成長主力，PC
需求則可能年減。

### Page 5

台灣 華碩

十一月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

五月 13, 2025 
ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

## Document 568: 凱基投顧_DIS Disney_賴偉中_20261119

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_DIS Disney_賴偉中_20261119.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 19, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 娛樂‧美國

Disney 
(DIS.US/DIS US)

營運穩健成長，重獲資金青睞  
重要訊息 
迪士尼公布 4Q26 財報，調整稀釋後 EPS 為 1.77 元，高於市場共識 6.9%。 
評論及分析 
4Q26 獲利年增 57%，三大事業同時成長。迪士尼 4Q26 營業利益 40.7 億
元、年增 56.5%，EPS 為 1.77 元、YoY+59.5%，略優於市場預期 6.9%。
娛樂、體驗、體育三大事業貢獻獲利分別成長 92%、20%、53%。其中娛
樂部門成長主要來自 SVOD 串流業務，貢獻獲利成長至 8.2 億元、
YoY+105%、QoQ+14%，而體育部門成長則來自 NFL 及大學美式足球賽的
收視表現亮眼，帶動訂閱及廣告收入增加。 
2027 年展望正向，串流仍扮演獲利成長主力。我們對迪士尼 2027 年展望
正向，首先是體驗部門，巴黎迪士尼於 1Q26 啟用冰雪奇緣世界帶來強勁人
次成長，預期可延續至 2027 年，同時 2027 年華特迪士尼世界(WDW)也規
畫啟用怪獸電力公司及熱帶美洲主題園區，整體樂園獲利可望維持成長。娛
樂部門成長仍來自 SVOD，除訂閱人數成長外，Disney+及 Hulu 整合為單一
App 的工程已接近完成，上線後預期可增加用戶黏性，預估娛樂部門貢獻營
業利益可成長至 80.2 億元、YoY+31.9%。最後是體育部門，除 NFL 及大學
美式足球賽轉播外，EPSN 也已取得 2027 年美國超級盃(Super Bowl)的轉播
權，將聯合 ABC 頻道與 Disney+平台一起轉播，預期可帶動 ESPN 訂閱用戶
成長，預估貢獻營業利益為 33.5 億元、YoY+8.4%。 
投資建議 
AI 敘事備受考驗的投資環境下，營運穩定成長的百年老店迪士尼重新獲得投
資人青睞，帶動股價自 8 月低點上漲超過 30%。目前股價 122.4 元約交易
於 15.4 倍 2027 年本益比，位於歷史區間 13~24 倍中下緣。考量迪士尼獲
利處於上行階段，評價仍具修復空間，我們維持迪士尼「增加持股」投資評
等，目標價由 136 元上修至 141 元，評價維持 2027 年 18 倍本益比。 
投資風險 
疫情再起、郵輪及樂園意外事故、電影票房不佳、有線電視業務衰退加速。

圖 4：公司概況  圖 5：季營收及年增率 
Disney 成立於 1923 年，為全球最大的娛樂及媒體集團之一，並
被納入道瓊工業指數成分股、標普 500 指數成分股及那斯達克指
數成分股。迪士尼主要業務分為體驗(樂園、郵輪)、娛樂(影視、
媒體、串流平台)、運動(體育節目)。截至 2025 財年底迪士尼於
全球共有 6 個主題遊樂園區(含自有、JV 及授權)、6 艘自有郵
輪，並有 Disney+、Hulu、ESPN+等三個串流平台。旗下大型影
視 工作室包括 Pixar 、 WaltDisney 、 Marvel 、 Lucasfilm 、 20 
century studios、abc、National geographic 等。

股價說明 等級 定義 
 增加持股 (OP) 對個股持正面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現超越凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 持有 (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 降低持股 (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 未評等 (NR) 凱基證券未對該個股加以評等。 
 受法規限制 
未評等 (R) 
受凱基證券集團內部政策和/或相關法令限制使凱基證券集團無法進行某些形式的資訊交流，其
中包括提供評等給投資人參考。 
  *總報酬 = (十二個月目標價-現價)/現價

法說會重點摘要

美時 
(1795 TT, NT$141.5, 增加持股)

重點摘要 
1. 美時 1-9M26 營收 248.18 億元，YoY +74.2%，主要受惠 Alvogen
併表與 B2B 業務；調整後 EBITDA 82.98 億元，YoY +35%。惟高
毛利 Lenalidomide 占比下降及韓國 Qsymia 轉為直銷，毛利率降至
50.8%（YoY -8.5ppts），加上併購融資推升財務成本，前三季
EPS 4.18 元，YoY -69.4%，3Q26 單季每股虧損 0.41 元 
2. 短期焦點為兩項美國重點產品，2025 年美國合計市場約 30 億美
元，已完成上市準備、僅待 FDA 最 終 核 准 ， 時 程較 原 先 預 期 的
2H26 遞延。另 Diazoxide Choline 緩釋錠為唯一首家申請者，有望
取得 180 天 獨 占 期 ； LP757 （ Cabozantinib ） PDUFA 日期為
2026/12/23。公司表示若兩項產品於 2026 年底前上市，全年毛利
率仍可達 55%以上 
3. 長期透過 Alvotech 合作，生物相似藥產品線擴至 11 項專案、潛在
市場約 310 億美元，美國 2029 年起、亞太 2027 年起陸續推出；
亞 洲 則 以 菲 律 賓 業 務 與 韓 國 UroVaxom 擴 大 布 局 。 在 淨 負 債 ／
EBITDA 達 3.35 倍下，美國新品上市帶來的現金流是降低財務成
本、修復獲利的關鍵 
結論 
美時前三季帳面獲利下滑主要來自併購融資成本，而非本業轉弱，營
業利益仍 YoY +16.8%。兩項美國重點產品取得 FDA 核准後，可同步
改善毛利率、現金流與融資條件，成為獲利回升的轉折點；FDA 核准
時點為關鍵驗證點，若延至 1Q27，財務成本下降亦將遞延。中長期則
有 Diazoxide Choline 180 天獨占期、LP757 早於學名藥上市，以及生
物相似藥與東南亞布局接棒，成長動能具延續性

前三季財務表現 
 1-9M26 營收 248.18 億元，YoY +74.2%；3Q26 營收 73.65 億
元，YoY +54%，主要受惠 Alvogen 併表與 B2B 業務 
 毛利率 50.8%，YoY -8.5ppts，主因高毛利的美國 Lenalidomide
占比下降，以及韓國 Qsymia 轉為直銷模式產生一次性影響；惟營
運規模擴大，毛利金額仍較去年同期增加約 41.65 億元 
 營業費用 74.58 億元，YoY +85.0%，主因併入 Alvogen；營業利
益 51.50 億元，YoY +16.8%，營益率 20.8%（去年同期 31.0%） 
 財務成本 36.11 億元（去年同期 3.18 億元），加上美元升值產生匯
損，淨業外費用約 35.9 億元；另 2025 年認列之 Alvogen US 特別
股公允價值利益 2026 年未再發生 
 1-9M26 稅後淨利 11.01 億元、EPS 4.18 元，皆 YoY -69.4%；
3Q26 單季淨損 1.08 億元、每股虧損 0.41 元

區域營收與產品組合 
 1-9M26 營收較去年同期增加約 106 億元，各區域變化： 
 美國增加 92.28 億元，YoY +172%，主要反映 Alvogen 併購 
 B2B 增加 12.40 億元，YoY +192% ，由 Nintedanib 與
Enzalutamide 帶動，並有多個市場新上市產品挹注 
 亞洲 YoY +1% ：韓國減少 2.09 億元（Qsymia 銷售模式轉
換）、台灣減少 0.17 億元（年度健保藥價調整）、東南亞增加
3.00 億元（泰國、越南及新併入的菲律賓業務） 
 區域營收占比為美國 59%、韓國 18%、台灣 8%、B2B 8%、東南
亞 7%；產品別為學名藥 75%、品牌藥 21%、生物相似藥 4%；
治療領域以腫瘤及免疫學、基礎醫護及生活品質各占 30%最大

財務槓桿與融資安排 
 2026/9 底淨負債／LTM 調整後 EBITDA 為 3.35 倍（2025/9 底為
0.83 倍），主要來自 Alvogen 收購及其併購融資 
 海外可轉換公司債（ECB）發行期間延長至 2027/2/3，資本結構調
整將配合重點產品上市時程，以爭取較佳融資條件 
 最大股東 PTT 透過子公司 Innobic 提供 1 億美元股東借款額度，可
用於償還債務，作為再融資期間的流動性緩衝

美國重點產品上市進度 
 兩項美國重點產品已完成開發、送件並獲 FDA 受理，商業化準備就
緒，僅待 FDA 最終核准；2025 年美國合計市場規模約 30 億美元
（IQVIA），瞄準學名藥競爭有限的成熟市場 
 其中子公司 Norwich 的 Rifaximin 學名藥於 8M26 獲美國聯邦上訴
法院有利判決，最終核准為剩餘的關鍵步驟 
 Diazoxide Choline 緩釋錠（VYKAT XR 學名藥）： 
 9M26 獲 FDA 受理，美時為唯一首家申請藥廠，有望取得 180
天學名藥市場獨占期，惟須視 FDA 核准與獨占期資格而定 
 VYKAT XR 用於小胖威利症，2025 年上市首年美國營收 1.9 億美
元，2Q26 銷售 9,400 萬美元，YoY +187% ； Neurocrine 
Biosciences 於 5M26 以 29 億美元收購以 VYKAT XR 為唯一商業
化產品的 Soleno Therapeutics 
 LP757（Cabozantinib）：  
 以 505(b)(2) 路徑申請，5M26 獲 FDA 受理，PDUFA 日期
2026/12/23，目標早於學名藥進入市場；3Q26 已於歐盟送件，
計畫比照 Lenalidomide 全球推出

線 
 UroVaxom：10M26 完成韓國產品權利收購，為預防反覆性泌尿道
感染的口服免疫刺激劑，公司不預期面臨學名藥競爭，預期 2027
年貢獻韓國營收約 3,000 萬美元，並洽談其他亞洲市場 
 Alvotech：8M26 取得 Durvalumab（原廠 Imfinzi）與 Emicizumab
（原廠 Hemlibra）生物相似藥美國非獨家及亞太 8 個市場獨家權
利，兩原廠藥 2025 年全球銷售各約 60 億美元 
 生物相似藥產品線增至 11 項專案（8 種分子），潛在市場約 310 億
美元：美國 5 項、市場約 290 億美元，目標 2029 年起陸續推出；
亞太 6 項、市場約 15 億美元，目標 2027 年起陸續推出 
 VIZZ（Aceclidine）為首款也是唯一以 aceclidine 為成分的每日一次
老花眼藥水，美時擁有韓國及 7 個東南亞市場獨家權利，2025/12
向 韓 國 MFDS 送 件 ， 目 標 2027 年 上 市 ， 年 度 最 高 銷 售 潛 力 逾
3,000 萬美元

QA 
 Lenalidomide 是否仍是重要貢獻來源？ 
 仍是營收與獲利的重要來源，惟併入 Alvogen 後占集團營收比重
下降；競爭持續加劇，公司預期價格壓力將延續 
 Alvogen 的 貢 獻 使 公 司 在 此 逆 風 下 ， 營 收 、 毛 利 與 調 整 後
EBITDA 仍能成長 
 2026 年全年毛利率展望？ 
 毛 利 率 的 明 顯 改 善 仍 有 賴 美 國 重 點 產 品 上 市 ； 若 兩 項 產 品 於
2026 年底前上市，公司認為全年毛利率可達 55%以上 
 重點美國產品是否遞延？上市後貢獻多快出現？ 
 時程較原先預期的 2H26 晚，核准時點取決於 FDA；目前已在最
後一步，商業化準備就緒，取得核准即可上市 
 公司預期上市後很快開始貢獻營收與獲利，新增的現金流入有助
於融資談判取得較佳條件

### Page 4

台灣 美時

十一月 13, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 4

 若上市延至 2027 年，融資計畫與財務成本下降是否同步遞延？ 
 公司預期新品上市可產生可觀現金、降低美國的整體負債規模，
因此於上市後處理剩餘未償債務最為有利；ECB 發行期間延至
2027/2 亦是為配合此計畫 
 公 司承認 若上 市延 至 1Q27， 財務 成本 下降的 時點亦 將隨之 遞
延，但上市後對融資金額與結構會有更大彈性 
 若不發行 ECB，再融資是否仍能進行？ 
 ECB 仍是融資計畫中有價值的選項，公司以整體角度規劃，方案
同時涵蓋有 ECB 與沒有 ECB 的情境

## Document 589: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_8155 博智_林祐熙_20261112

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_Takeaway_8155 博智_林祐熙_20261112.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

法說會議重點摘要

博智 
(8155 TT, NT$255, 未評等)

重點摘要 
1. 3Q26毛利率季增 3 ppts 至 26.9%，來自於高階產品出貨比重拉升，
漲價效應。 
2. 2027 年展望，AI Server 增加、 IPC: B/B >1、IC Tester: 機種、客戶增
加。 
3. 2028 年 AI Server 需求持續強勁， IC Tester 比例增加，低軌衛星與太
空 AI 伺服器專案，博智具備極高機會參與高階板材製造。 
結論 
我們認為公司在 AI 伺服器與高毛利 IC Tester 比重提升下將大幅受惠，目
前股價交易於 2027 市場共識之每股盈餘的 11.3 倍。 
營運概況 
 1Q-3Q26 累計合併營收達 48 億元，較 2025 年同期成長 35%。
2026 年單季營收呈逐季增長，累計 1Q-3Q26 年增達三成以上。累
計每股盈餘達到 11.15 元。3Q26 毛利率跳升，因高階產品出貨比
重拉升，且成功將上游 CCL 材料漲價順利轉嫁給客戶。 
 2026 產品結構，Server 營收占比 82%、IC tester 9% 、IPC 7%及
Networking 1%。 
 2026 板層數營收佔比：14-20L、 22-34+L、 2-12L 以上佔比為
50%、27%、23%。 
 博智核心技術：  
 實際量產中非對稱堆疊（N+M）設計顯著增加，極易引發板彎
翹。 
 需在高層數、大尺寸及板彎翹條件下精準控制背鑽深度，加工數
千至數萬個不同深度之孔位。公司具備高重橫比電鍍及 Multi-
layer 集成能力。 
 博智核心 SI（訊號完整性）模擬實力：，CCL 材料交期拉長
時，博智憑藉強大的 SI 模擬能力，能協助客戶快速尋找並驗證 
Second source 備用材料，大幅降低認證門檻與缺料衝擊。 
 目標市場現況：  
 Server：主流聚焦約 8-30 層板，排版尺寸最大達 22x27 吋，板
厚不超過 5mm，採用 M6-7 材料。受限於現有產能，優先配置
生產非高階 HDI 之 N+M 及 2/3 次壓合高層板。 
 IPC：應用於工業、醫療與機器人，層數 2-26 層，尺寸 20x24 
吋，板厚約 3.0mm，採用 M4-6 材料。(訂單能見度:6-12 個月
訂單)。

## Document 609: 史上最大IPO开盘狂热不足一小时，Anthropic首秀涨34%后回吐半数涨幅，市值一度突破2万亿

Source PDF: all report pdf/史上最大IPO开盘狂热不足一小时，Anthropic首秀涨34%后回吐半数涨幅，市值一度突破2万亿.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic IPO 公开募资 813 亿美元，收盘完全稀释估值 1.77 万亿；开盘涨 34% 、市值全球
第六，到收盘涨 17% 、市值落后于 SpaceX ⾸秀时；⾸分钟成交 150 亿美元，半⼩时内较开
盘价跌 13% 、创 2008 年来美股⼤型 IPO 最差开局。 CEO 敲钟时承诺不屈服于商业和政治压
⼒，强调 AI 安全及医学突破。华尔街对 AI 前景仍有分歧，虽然此 IPO 被誉为 “ ⾕歌时刻 ” ，但
⾯临估值与盈利考验，未来数⽉股价表现才是 AI 投资热情试⾦⽯。
摘要：
AI 独⻆兽 Anthropic 刷新了 SpaceX 五个⽉前才创下的上市募资纪录，登陆美股开盘⼤涨幅度超过
暗盘预计最⾼⽔平、将公司市值推升⾄ 2 万亿美元以上，但狂热很快 “ 退烧 ” 。股价开盘后半⼩时
内较开盘价回落 13% 、创 2008 年以来⼤型美股 IPO 的最差半⼩时开局。
美东时间 11 ⽉ 19 ⽇周四， Anthropic 以 ANT 为股票代码正式在纳斯达克挂牌交易，开盘报 111.50

美元，较 IPO 发⾏价涨约 34.3% ，盘中最⾼触及 115.10 美元，涨幅扩⼤到约 38.7% ，此后持续回
吐涨幅，刷新⽇低时回到⾄ 92.80 美元下⽅、涨幅收窄⾄不⾜ 12% ，到收盘，较发⾏价涨约
17.1% ，相当于回吐开盘时的半数涨幅，盘后还较收盘价下跌。
此次 Anthropic IPO 公开募资约 813 亿美元，刷新全球 IPO 募资规模纪录。投资者对 Anthropic 的
追捧，也为 AI ⾏业提供了⼀个重要的资本市场⻛向标。
从⾸⽇交易看，创纪录募资证明资本市场仍愿意为头部 AI 企业提供巨额资⾦。开盘半⼩时后迅速
回吐涨幅说明， AI ⻰头光环固然带来了强劲的初始需求，却没有消除投资者对⾼估值、巨额算⼒
投⼊及未来盈利能⼒的疑虑。 Anthropic 后市表现将成为衡量整个 AI 投资周期热度和可持续性的
重要观察窗⼝。
813 亿美元 IPO 公开募资创纪录，收盘完全稀释估值约 1.77 万亿美元
美东时间 11 ⽉ 19 ⽇， Anthropic 以股票代码 ANT 在纳斯达克正式开始交易。公司此前将发⾏价定

为每股 83 美元，⾼于 70 ⾄ 78 美元的发⾏指导区间，公开发⾏募资约 813 亿美元，超过 SpaceX 此
前约 750 亿美元的 IPO 募资纪录，成为史上规模最⼤的 IPO 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

此外， Anthropic 还通过同步私募配售筹集约 260 亿美元，整个交易规模合计约 1073 亿美元。需
要区分的是，创纪录的 IPO 公开募资额为 813 亿美元，⽽ 1073 亿美元是将私募配售⼀并计⼊后的
整体融资规模。
按照 83 美元发⾏价计算， Anthropic 上市时的估值约为 1.5 万亿美元。上市前的暗盘交易释放强烈
的看涨信号。德国券商 Lang & Schwarz 此前给出的报价⼀度达到 105 美元，较发⾏价溢价
26% ；加密货币平台 Hyperliquid 上与 Anthropic 挂钩的永续期货也曾⼤幅⾼于发⾏价。
开盘后 Anthropic 股价表现还远超暗盘预⽰⽔平。以开盘价计算，公司市值曾突破 2 万亿美元。以
这⼀盘中市值看， Anthropic 成⽴才五年多便跻⾝全球最⾼市值的上市公司前⼗，位列苹果、微
软、英伟达、⾕歌⺟公司 Alphabet 和亚⻢逊之后。
换⾔之， Anthropic 开盘市值超过了美股 “ 科技七巨头 ” （ Magnificent 7 ）中的两家 —— 特斯拉和

Meta ，仅次于其他五巨头，也超过 SpaceX 上市⾸⽇开盘价对应的约 1.96 万亿美元估值。
但 Anthropic 的股价⾼涨势头未能持续，最终收报 97.17 美元。以完全稀释⼝径计算，到收盘时，
公司估值约为 1.77 万亿美元。按收盘价， Anthropic 的市值则未能超越 SpaceX 上市⾸⽇⽔平。
尽管股价较盘中⾼点明显回落， Anthropic 收盘估值仍处于全球⾼市值企业之列。
⾸分钟成交约 150 亿美元，但半⼩时表现创 08 年以来美股⼤型 IPO 最差开局
Anthropic 上市初期的交易热度极⾼。雅虎财经数据显⽰，⾸笔 111.50 美元的交易涉及约 1.08 亿
股，成交⾦额约 120 亿美元；上市后的第⼀个完整交易分钟，成交额进⼀步达到约 150 亿美元。
这⼀成交规模远超此前多家⼤型 IPO 。雅虎财经计算， Anthropic ⾸笔交易⾦额已超过 SpaceX 今
年 6 ⽉上市⾸分钟约 90 亿美元的成交额；⾸个完整交易分钟的成交额则接近 SpaceX 的两倍，约

为阿⾥巴巴 2014 年上市⾸分钟成交额的三倍。
但买盘热度很快转向抛售。雅虎财经根据 21 家⼤型美国 IPO 的分钟级交易数据进⾏⽐较后指出，
Anthropic 上市后的前半⼩时表现，是 2008 年以来⼤型美国 IPO 中最差的⼀次。
具体来看， Anthropic 股价从 111.50 美元的⾸笔交易价格跌⾄约 97 美元，半⼩时内下跌约 13% 。
这⼀表现略逊于电商公司 Coupang 上市半⼩时后的跌幅；作为对照， SpaceX 上市半⼩时后股价
仍上涨近 10% 。

### Page 3

此后股价继续震荡下探，盘中最低跌⾄ 92.72 美元，较 83 美元发⾏价仅上涨约 11.7% 。相较开盘
时 34.3% 的涨幅，股价⼀度⼏乎抹去⼤部分初始溢价。收盘时股价回升⾄ 97.17 美元，但仍较
111.50 美元的⾸笔交易价格低约 12.8% 。
换⾔之， Anthropic 最终收盘涨幅虽然仍有 17.1% ，但相较开盘时 34.3% 的涨幅，已经回吐约⼀
半。雅虎财经还估算，开盘后半⼩时的下跌⼀度令公司市值减少近 2000 亿美元。
这场交易也凸显出 IPO ⾸⽇价格与⻓期估值之间的差异：⾸笔交易和开盘初期的⾼涨反映了投资
者对稀缺 AI 资产的追捧，⽽随后快速回落则显⽰，资⾦并未在⾼位形成持续⼀致的买⼊意愿。
CEO 敲钟承诺不屈服于商业和政治压⼒，强调 AI 安全及医学突破
在纳斯达克上市仪式上， Anthropic CEO 达⾥奥 · 阿莫代伊（ Dario Amodei ）、公司总裁兼联合创
始⼈丹妮拉 · 阿莫代伊（ Daniela Amodei ）及其他联合创始⼈和⾼管共同敲响开市钟。

Anthropic 团队敲钟仪式截图来⾃纳斯达克
阿莫代伊在讲话中再次强调 AI 安全是公司的核⼼使命，并作出明确承诺： Anthropic 将根据安全
情况决定 AI 模型的训练和部署节奏，不会为了商业利益或政治压⼒⽽牺牲安全标准。
他表⽰：
他还强调，此次上市让 Anthropic 处于更强⼤的财务地位，但这只会进⼀步坚定公司坚持正确发
展道路的决⼼。如果能够证明企业既可以坚持安全原则，⼜能够取得商业上的巨⼤成功，其他 AI
公司也会受到激励。他将这种良性竞争称为 “ 向上的竞赛 ” （ Race to the Top ）。
除了安全问题，阿莫代伊还描绘了 AI 在医学研究领域的潜⼒。他表⽰， Claude 已经帮助蛋⽩质
折叠、药物设计、癌症遗传学和临床试验管理等领域的研究⼈员⼤幅缩短研究时间。
他认为，⾜够强⼤的 AI 如果能够与⼈类协作，有可能将⼀个世纪的科学和医学进步压缩到⼗年。
不过，他也提醒，⽬前取得的仍是早期成果，实验和临床试验依然需要时间。
“ 我们将控制 AI 系统的训练和部署节奏，只要有必要，就会持续投⼊时间，直到我们有信⼼确认

这些系统是安全的，并且能够始终处于⼈类控制之下。我们不会屈服于商业压⼒，也不会屈服于
政治压⼒。 ”

### Page 4

丹妮拉 · 阿莫代伊则强调， Anthropic 从创⽴之初就将 AI 安全置于重要位置，公司希望证明，负责
任地开发 AI 与取得商业成功并不⽭盾。
年化运营收⼊ 810 亿美元，算⼒和基础设施承诺接近 9000 亿
Anthropic 的⾼估值建⽴在快速增⻓的业务基础之上。公司披露，截⾄ 2026 年 10 ⽉ 31 ⽇，年化运
营收⼊已达到约 810 亿美元，较 9 ⽉底增加约 100 亿美元。第三季度收⼊为 169.56 亿美元，较第
⼆季度增⻓约 47% 。
企业客⼾扩张同样迅速。截⾄ 9 ⽉底，过去 12 个⽉内为 Anthropic 贡献⾄少 10 万美元收⼊的企业
客⼾已超过 1.19 万家，⽽ 2025 年底这⼀数字约为 1500 家。过去 12 个⽉收⼊达到 100 万美元以上
的企业客⼾也已超过 1900 家。
但⾼速增⻓尚未转化为稳定盈利。 Anthropic 第三季度经营亏损约 5.67 亿美元，⽽第⼆季度曾实
现约 9.74 亿美元经营利润。公司解释，第三季度经营亏损主要受到 50 亿美元短期算⼒承诺成

本，以及 20 亿美元⾮现⾦捐赠配捐费⽤影响。
算⼒成本的快速上升是重要原因。 Anthropic 第三季度算⼒与基础设施⽀出达到约 99 亿美元，较
第⼆季度的约 56 亿美元⼤幅增加； 2026 年前三季度相关⽀出合计约 188 亿美元。尽管公司通过
模型效率提升和收⼊增⻓改善了整体经营表现，但算⼒仍是其最⼤的成本项⽬。
未来投⼊压⼒同样不容忽视。 Anthropic 在招股书中披露，截⾄ 2026 年 9 ⽉底及之后签订的采购
承诺和租赁协议总额约为 9009 亿美元，其中约 4516 亿美元预计在 2030 年底前发⽣，其余⼤部分
预计在 2031 年⾄ 2045 年间发⽣。
这意味着，市场不仅需要判断 Anthropic 能否继续扩⼤企业客⼾和收⼊规模，也需要评估其能否
让收⼊增⻓持续覆盖算⼒、数据中⼼和其他基础设施成本。尤其值得注意的是， Anthropic 的收
⼊主要来⾃按使⽤量计费的业务，⽽公司签订的算⼒和基础设施协议往往具有多年期限和最低
采购义务，收⼊波动与⻓期固定投⼊之间的错配，正是投资者关注的⻛险之⼀。

华尔街对 AI 前景仍有分歧： “ ⾕歌时刻 ” 背后是估值与盈利考验
对于 Anthropic 上市的⾏业意义，华尔街⼈⼠给出了不同解读。
Bessemer Venture Partners 合伙⼈ Byron Deeter 将这次 IPO 称为 Anthropic 的 “ ⾕歌时刻 ” （ Google
moment ）。他认为， Anthropic 不必像⾕歌曾经主导搜索市场那样独占 AI ，未来 AI ⽣态有望容纳
多家优秀企业， Anthropic 有机会继续成为前沿模型领域的市场领导者。
⽽对于⾸⽇股价表现，市场⼈⼠提醒投资者不要将开盘溢价简单视为⻓期价值的证明。
F.L. Putnam Investment Management ⾸席市场策略师 Ellen Hazen 认为， Anthropic ⾯临⻓期且
不可取消的基础设施负债，⽽收⼊中约⼋成来⾃按使⽤量计费的业务，可能随客⼾使⽤情况变
化。她认为，公司⻓期负债与相对短期、可能波动的收⼊之间存在期限错配。
Hazen 指出， Anthropic 的收⼊增⻓具有软件公司的特征，但资产负债表却更像⼀家资本密集型

⼯业企业：公司需要持续投⼊巨额资⾦建设基础设施，却⽆法保证客⼾需求和市场份额始终保
持当前⽔平。她认为，以软件公司的估值倍数衡量⼀家拥有重资产投⼊的企业，并不⼀定合
理。
Great Hill Capital 董事⻓ Thomas Hayes 则认为， Anthropic 是⼀家增⻓惊⼈的公司，但当前股价
已经很⾼。他表⽰，投资者对公司前景的热情仍可能延续⼀段时间，不过未来⼀年可能出现更

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

好的买⼊机会。
Capital Economics 市场经济学家 Joe Maher 也提醒，不应过度解读新股上市初期的价格跳涨。
他指出，新股早期交易往往波动较⼤， Anthropic 未来数⽉的股价表现才是观察投资者 AI 热情的
重要试⾦⽯。
Maher 认为， Anthropic 等 AI 实验室是连接终端消费者和企业需求与底层 AI 供应链的重要渠道，
其算⼒和数据中⼼⽀出直接关系到云计算巨头和半导体公司的前景。与此同时， Anthropic 也正
在从 AI 供应链的客⼾逐渐成为部分环节的竞争者，例如计划发展⾃有数据中⼼和芯⽚设计能⼒。
Yorkville Ives & Co. 合伙⼈兼董事总经理 Dan Ives 则表⽰，尽管 Anthropic 完成了规模空前的

IPO ，市场仍对半导体⾏业和中国相关问题保持警惕，科技股正处于⼀段需要重新检验市场信⼼
的时期。
对于 Anthropic ⽽⾔，创纪录 IPO 只是进⼊公开市场的第⼀步。随着公司开始接受季度财报、盈
利能⼒和资本开⽀的持续检验，其股价能否维持⾼估值，最终仍取决于企业客⼾付费需求能否
持续增⻓，以及庞⼤的算⼒投⼊能否转化为可持续的商业回报。

短期風險尚存，謹慎擇優市場
投

市場展望投資策略
 短期風險資產重新訂價，關注法債：近期股市回檔推升
債券價格，美債殖利率大幅回落，把握5%殖利率水準分
批布局中長天期美國公債或投資等級債ETF。另建議關
注法債，當前與德債的利差具收斂空間，且相對估值便
宜，回測當前殖利率水準作為長期買點勝率100%。(詳
見Page 12、13)
 逢回加碼科技股、儲能工業：AI投資疑慮再起，科技股
財報考驗市場，引發科技股震盪。AI因推論以及企業應
用推升電力，傳統工業巨頭深耕新品滿足AI高壓電力需
求，關注廣泛的AI受惠儲能工業。(詳見Page 16、17)
 GPU租賃價格續高，韓國及台灣股市受惠：儘管AI需求
受到質疑，但數據顯示，在會計6年折舊準則之下，
GPU資產市場價值卻遠高於公允價值，反映AI真實需求
帶來的AI晶片維持高溢價，且海力士記憶體財報優於預
期，關注受AI晶片受惠的台韓國家股市相關類股。(詳見
Page 19)
 匯市展望：近期外匯市場相對股債平靜，歐元受經濟數

據及法國政治波動影響呈現結構性疲軟，美元因通膨持
續攀升及未來升息步伐明確，在FOMC會議後將持續重
新訂價，預估美元指數強勢走揚，本周美元指數區間看
在99.50~102.5。
 債市展望：近期股市AI敘事受到挑戰，資金迅速轉向防
禦類資產，債券殖利率短期在各國升息政策下迅速回落，
預期將會持續影響債市，本周10年美債殖利率區間看在
4.90%~5.05%。
 股市展望: CSP財報表現分歧，拖累整體大盤表現，且期
中選舉仍有不確定風險，指數可能出現震盪整理，策略
上應採取選股不選市，著重基本面，本周標普500指數
區間看在7,550~7,700。

利率及地緣風險緩解，財報陸續出爐
4
市
場
